5.11 Reservations

Reservations operate as defined in the NVM Express Base Specification with the additional I/O Command
Set specific Command Behavior in the Presence of a Reservation defined in Figure 199.




                                                    172
NVM Express® NVM Command Set Specification, Revision 1.3



                    Figure 199: Command Behavior in the Presence of a Reservation

                                                                                                         Write Exclusive                 Exclusive Access
                                                                                                         Registrants Only                Registrants Only
                                            Write                           Exclusive
                                                                                                                or                              or
                                          Exclusive                          Access
                                                                                                         Write Exclusive                 Exclusive Access

5.12 Sanitize Operations

Sanitize operates as defined in the NVM Express Base Specification. NVM Command Set specific
definitions and extensions are defined in this section.
In addition to the requirements in the NVM Express Base Specification, the following NVM Command Set
Admin commands (refer to Figure 200) are allowed while a sanitize operation is in progress:

                      Figure 200: Sanitize Operations – Admin Commands Allowed
   Admin Command         Additional Restrictions
                         The log pages listed below are allowed in addition to the log pages listed in the NVM Express
                         Base Specification.
                           Log Pages                                           Additional Restrictions
     Get Log Page                                     Return zeroes in the LBA field (refer to section 4.1.4.1). To prevent
                           Error                      recovery of user data, other fields in the log page are modified as required
                           Information                by the Sanitize Operations section in the NVM Express Base
                                                      Specification.




                                                                          173
                                              NVM Express® NVM Command Set Specification, Revision 1.3


Following a successful sanitize operation, the values of user data (including protection information, and
non-PI metadata) that result from an audit (refer to the NVM Express Base Specification) of the sanitization
target are specified in Figure 201.
If the controller does not deallocate logical blocks as part of processing a sanitize operation, then following
a successful completion of that sanitize operation:
      •   if the controller processes a read command specifying protection information checking (i.e., the
          PRCHK field is set to a non-zero value or the STC bit is set to ‘1’) before a write operation is
          performed on the logical blocks specified by that read command, then the controller may abort that
          read command with a status code indicating a protection information check error (refer to the Media
          and Data Integrity Errors Definition section of the NVM Express Base Specification).
If the controller deallocates logical blocks as part of processing a sanitize operation, then after successful
completion of that sanitize operation, the values read from deallocated logical blocks are described in
section 3.3.3.2.1. The host may specify that sanitized logical blocks not be deallocated by setting the No-
Deallocate After Sanitize bit to ‘1’ in the Sanitize command.

                           Figure 201: Sanitize Operation Types – User Data Values
      Sanitize Operation
                              User Data
             Type
         Block Erase          Vendor specific value
        Crypto Erase          Indeterminate value
                              Refer to Sanitize Operations – Overwrite Mechanism in the NVM Express Base
          Overwrite
                              Specification

          Media Verification
While the sanitization target is in the Media Verification state (refer to the Sanitize Operation State Machine
Section in the NVM Express Base Specification), the controller processes Read commands as described
in this section and shall not abort those commands with a status code of Sanitize In Progress or Sanitize
Namespace In Progress.
If:
      •   the controller processes a Read command that does not specify any Protection Information (PI)
          checking (i.e., the PRCHK field is cleared to 000b (refer to Figure 11) and the STC bit is cleared to
          ‘0’ (refer to Figure 12));
      •   each LBA specified by that Read command is in a sanitization target that is in the Media Verification
          state (i.e., is in the NVM subsystem for a sanitization target of the NVM subsystem or is in the
          namespace for a sanitization target of a namespace); and
      •   for each LBA specified by that command for which media is allocated, the controller is able to read
          data from the media,
then:
      •   for each LBA specified by that command for which media is allocated, the controller:
              o   shall ignore data integrity errors, if any (e.g., shall not abort that command with a status
                  code of Unrecovered Read Error if the controller is able to read that media);
              o   shall return data that is read from that media; and
              o   may return different data for successive reads (i.e., without any writes between those
                  reads) of the same LBA (e.g., to obscure media reliability);
      •   for each LBA specified by that command for which media is not allocated, the controller shall return
          data or abort that command with a status code of Deallocated or Unwritten Logical Block as
          described in section 3.3.3.2.1; and




                                                      174
NVM Express® NVM Command Set Specification, Revision 1.3


    •   the controller shall complete that command with a status code of Successful Media Verification
        Read if the command has not been aborted with a different status code (e.g., Deallocated or
        Unwritten Logical Block).
If the controller processes a Read command that does not specify any PI checking and is unable to read
the data from the media for any LBA specified by that command for which media is allocated, then the
controller shall abort the command with a status code of Unrecovered Read Error.
If the controller processes a Read command specifying PI checking (i.e., the PRCHK field is set to a non-
zero value or the STC bit is set to ‘1’), then the controller shall abort that command with a status code of
Invalid Field in Command.

5.13 Streams

Streams operate as defined in the NVM Express Base Specification. The unit of granularity for the NVM
Command Set specific definition of the Stream Write Size (SWS) field is in logical blocks.

                                                      NVM Express® NVM Command Set Specification, Revision 1.3


Appendix A Rate Limiting Capability Implementation
This section describes an example of using multiple token buckets (refer to Computer Networks, Fifth
Edition) a method for implementing the Rate Limiting capability (refer to section 5.10). If implemented, a set
of token buckets should be used for:
        •   fetching commands for the target specified by the Target field (refer to Figure 106) in the Rate
            Limiting feature;
        •   processing candidate commands for the target specified by the Target field in the Rate Limiting
            feature; and
        •   for access to each internal resource within the processing of those candidate commands such
            as caches, media, etc.
The set of token buckets consists of a token bucket for:
        •   Total Bandwidth;
        •   Write Bandwidth;
        •   Total IOPS; and
        •   Write IOPS.
Figure 202 shows an example of a single token bucket for a specific internal resource. Tokens are added
to the token bucket at a vendor specific constant rate Δt and the number of tokens allowed in the token
bucket is capped based on the bandwidth or IOPS limit during Δt.

                                            Figure 202: Example Token Bucket

                                                                                  Every Δt tokens are added




                                                                 Token
                                                                 Bucket


                   Commands waiting for
                    the internal resource


                         Command
                                                                                    Yes
                         Command
                                                            Tokens available to
                           ...              Command
                                                           process a command?          Process
                                                                                    the command
                                                                                                     Internal Resource


                         Command




If there are enough tokens in the token bucket to process an entire command, then the processing of an
entire command is allowed to continue being processed for this particular token bucket.
If there are not enough tokens in the token bucket to process an entire command but there are tokens
available to process a portion of the command, then that portion of the command may be processed. For
example, if token bucket has enough tokens to process 5 LBAs then 5 LBAs of a Write command specifying
10 LBAs may be processed. Even though a portion of the command is processed, the command completion
is not allowed to be posted to the command completion entry until the entire command is processed.
If there are no tokens in a token bucket, then no command or a portion of that command is allowed to be
processed.
Commands that are not allowed to be processed because there are not enough tokens in a token bucket
are processed at a later time when tokens are added each Δt. Commands are not dropped if unable to be
processed due to not enough tokens available in a token bucket.




                                                              176
NVM Express® NVM Command Set Specification, Revision 1.3


A command is allowed to continue being processed if there are enough tokens available in each of the set
of token buckets for the internal resource. If a command is allowed to continue being processed, then tokens
are removed from each of the token bucket based on the type of command and the size of the command.
Section A.1 has an example of the tokens removed from the buckets for write commands. Section A.2 has
an example of the tokens removed from the buckets for read commands.
A.1 Write Command

    •   Remove tokens from the Total Bandwidth bucket based on the size of a write command times the
        Write to Read Bandwidth ratio (refer to Figure 107).
    •   Remove tokens from the Write Bandwidth bucket based on the size of a write command.
    •   Remove tokens from the Total IOPS bucket based on the Write to Read IOPS Ratio (refer to Figure
        107).
    •   Remove one token from the Write IOPS bucket.
A.2 Read Command

    •   Remove tokens from the Total Bandwidth bucket based on the size of a read command.
    •   Remove one token from the Total IOPS bucket.
